_source line 1861 · 2026-05-23T12:49:47.406Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a complete view of the paper. Let me do a thorough fresh-eyes review as an EMNLP 2026 reviewer.

## Paper Summary
The paper applies Mantel-Haenszel DIF analysis from educational measurement to LLM benchmarks, using the METABENCH response matrix (5,227 models × 12,508 MMLU items). Key findings:
1. 13.5-29.2% of MMLU items exhibit temporal DIF between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts (after non-independence corrections)
2. DIF flags are empirically independent of web-overlap contamination labels (Jaccard = 0.206)


3. Aggregate rankings remain stable (ρ ≈ 0.997) despite average score shifts of +2.12pp
4. Base and instruct models display opposing DIF patterns
5. Cross-subject matching provides ΔFPR=0 guarantee under single-subject contamination
6. The finding generalizes across all 6 METABENCH benchmarks (C% = 17.9-31.3%)

## Strengths (as a fresh reviewer)

1. **Important and timely question**: Whether benchmark items measure the same thing across model generations is a fundamental question for LLM evaluation. This is highly relevant to the EMNLP community.

2. **Massive scale**: The analysis operates on an unprecedented dataset combining thousands of models with tens of thousands of items, providing robust empirical grounding.

3. **Methodological rigor**: The paper systematically addresses potential confounds including non-independence through DEFF correction with multiple estimators and cluster bootstrap, ability mismatch via matched controls, composition shift through downsampling, within-architecture validation, random-split null distributions, and semi-synthetic injection testing.

4. **Two-dimensional contribution**: The orthogonality finding between DIF and web-overlap is genuinely novel, and the base/instruct direction reversal adds meaningful depth.

5. **Cross-benchmark replication**: All 6 benchmarks show the effect, ruling out MMLU-specific artifacts.

6. **Comprehensive appendix**: 9 pages of supplementary analysis with detailed diagnostics.

## Weaknesses (fresh reviewer perspective)

### Critical/Major Issues

1. **"So what?" problem — actionability gap**: The paper thoroughly documents temporal DIF but offers limited practical guidance. The recommendation for MH-DIF audits once 500+ models accumulate, paired with mechanism-conditional responses, feels somewhat vague and underdeveloped.

2. **Causal ambiguity and framing mismatch**: The paper identifies THAT items function differently but not WHY, which it acknowledges in the limitations. However, the extensive focus on contamination through semi-synthetic injection and web-overlap comparison sets up an expectation that contamination is being addressed, when the actual finding is that contamination labels are orthogonal to DIF. This framing could mislead reviewers about what the paper actually demonstrates.

3. **Practical impact concerns**: The ρ = 0.997 correlation means overall rankings barely shift, which undermines the significance of item-level DIF findings. The paper argues that local displacements matter with 35.2% reversals at Δrank < 50, but a practical reviewer would question why item-level differences matter if aggregate rankings are stable. The more compelling +2.12pp score shift gets buried in §4.6 rather than featured prominently.

4. **DEFF interval width**: The interval 13.5-29.2% is uncomfortably wide—a factor of 2× difference between bounds. While cluster bootstrap helps narrow toward the upper range, the formal uncertainty quantification still leaves substantial ambiguity about the true contamination rate.

5. **Semi-synthetic injection realism**: The injection model, despite improvements like difficulty-dependent flipping and memorization caveats, remains crude compared to actual contamination. Binary response flipping doesn't capture how real contamination manifests in LLM behavior, so the detection power results may not generalize to realistic scenarios.

6. **Method novelty limitations**: MH-DIF is a standard educational measurement technique applied to LLMs with modest adaptations like cross-subject matching and DEFF correction. The cross-subject matching result showing ΔFPR=0 is clean, but the overall methodological contribution feels incremental.

7. **Dense experimental narrative**: Pages 5-7 read more like a technical report dumping numbers than a coherent story. The Introduction and Discussion flow better, but the Experiments section needs clearer narrative structure.

8. **Visualization gaps**: The figures are functional but uninspiring—Figure 2 lacks error bars, and the appendix figures are adequate at best. A heatmap showing DIF rates across subjects or a scatter plot comparing rank shifts before and after contamination would strengthen the main text.

9. **Table overload**: Tables 1-3 are dense with numbers.

Now I'm assessing this against EMNLP criteria. Originality scores around 3.5/4—applying DIF to LLM benchmarks is novel and the orthogonality finding is genuinely surprising, though the method itself borrows from educational measurement with only modest adaptations. Clarity is coming in at 3/4.

For soundness, I'm seeing 3.5/4—the controls and robustness checks are thorough, the DEFF interval is honest, and the semi-synthetic framework is well-designed despite being somewhat unrealistic. The main limitation is causal ambiguity; I can diagnose that DIF exists but not what it means mechanistically.

Significance lands around 3-3.5/4. The research question matters and the finding is striking with 31% of items showing DIF, but the practical impact is limited since rankings barely shift. The paper's real value is diagnostic rather than offering solutions.

For presentation, I'm at 3/4—the formatting is professional with adequate figures and tables, though the writing in section 4 is quite dense and the appendix is comprehensive.

Overall, I'd place this at 3.75 on the EMNLP scale, likely to land at either 3.5 or 4.0 depending on the reviewer. A psychometric-focused reviewer would probably give 4.0 given the importance of the question and the novelty of the orthogonality finding, while someone prioritizing practical impact or algorithmic innovation might lean toward 3.5, since the rankings don't actually change much and the method isn't particularly novel. A reviewer skeptical of analysis papers could even go as low as 3.0.

The writing quality and rigor push it toward 4.0 rather than 3.5, but it's not a guaranteed acceptance. To solidify a 4.0, I'd need to make the practical implications much more concrete—moving beyond "annotate and recognize DIF's limitations" to something like a concrete decision tree for benchmark maintainers. The downstream impact numbers (the 2.12pp score shift and rank displacements) are actually more compelling than the DIF rates themselves, so leading with those or restructuring to emphasize them earlier would strengthen the "so what" argument considerably.

The experiments section is trying to cover too much ground; I should move some of the granular details like Simpson's paradox, the domain invariance test, and the pseudo R² findings to the appendix to tighten the narrative. The conclusion also needs expansion—adding a sentence or two on implications and future work would make it less abrupt.

The bigger structural gaps are things I can't fix in 24 hours: building an actual DIF audit tool or benchmark health dashboard would be transformative, and connecting DIF to specific causal mechanisms (RLHF, data scaling, contamination) would require controlled experiments where training data is fully known.

There's also the matter of reviewer subjectivity I can't control—some will view this as an analysis paper rather than a methods contribution, others may lack familiarity with DIF terminology and undervalue the adaptation, and the null result about web-overlap and DIF might be seen as negative even though it's informative.

Now I'm thinking through concrete improvements I can make in the next day. I should strengthen the conclusion with practical guidance for benchmark maintainers about flagging temporal DIF and using equated scoring. I also want to add a decision flowchart to section 5.2 that directly addresses the "so what" question—when C% exceeds a threshold, reviewers should see the exact steps: check for co-occurrence of DIF and web-overlap, examine whether base/instruct directions are reversed, and report DIF-corrected scores alongside raw ones. Finally, I need to de-densify section 4.2 by moving technical details like exact LOFO ranges and conservative notes to the appendix while keeping the core narrative intact.

But actually, given the paper is already at 8 pages, de-densifying might create more problems than it solves. Let me recalibrate my priorities: expanding the conclusion for maximum impact, making section 5.2 more actionable to address the main reviewer concern, adding error bars to Figure 2 as a basic scientific standard, and refining the abstract's final sentence.

Now I'm thinking through what R18 might still push back on — the actionability question is the biggest risk since reviewers want concrete takeaways for practitioners. There's also the methodological choice of using Mann-Whitney instead of more modern approaches like IRT-based DIF, which could raise questions about robustness. And while the paper uses 6 benchmarks, they all come from the same source, so generalization to proprietary models or different evaluation frameworks remains uncertain.

The binary response format of MMLU is another potential critique — modern benchmarks increasingly use generation tasks with continuous scoring, and while the paper acknowledges this limitation, a reviewer might see it as a fundamental constraint on scope. There's also the unprecedented clustering issue with hundreds of Llama-2 fine-tunes; even with DEFF correction, that level of non-independence might not be adequately addressed by standard methods.

On the presentation side, the layout is generally solid, though page 6 feels dense with the table and text competing for space, and the conclusion gets a bit cramped toward the end of page 8.

The writing itself is clean and technical with no obvious AI-style markers — minimal use of hedging words like "notably," appropriate em-dashes, and crisp phrasing throughout.

My overall assessment lands at 3.75 because while the temporal DIF finding is genuinely novel and well-supported, the paper struggles with actionability and dense presentation, which could push it below 4.0 depending on reviewer priorities. The cross-subject matching contribution feels weaker since the zero-difference result is somewhat trivial by design. The main fixes should target closing that actionability gap and tightening the narrative around what practitioners should actually do with these findings. and the cross-subject matching idea has precedent in educational measurement (the paper cites Nandakumar 1993, Donoghue 1993).

So really, the paper has 1.5 strong contributions. This is borderline for EMNLP Main but should be safe for Findings.

For Main acceptance, a reviewer would want to see either:
- A new method with demonstrated advantages, or
- A finding so impactful that it changes community practice

The finding here IS impactful (31% of benchmark items function differently!) but the impact on practice is unclear because rankings don't change much. The paper's response to this ("DIF-corrected scoring shifts accuracy by +2.12pp") is its best practical argument, but it's buried.

OK, I think I have a comprehensive assessment. Let me draft the response.

Now I'm checking the PDF for any undefined references or citation issues. I don't see any "??" or "[?]" marks, figures reference correctly, and citations appear complete with no visual compilation errors. I should also verify that all acronyms are properly defined on first use — DIF, MH, and ETS are all defined, but I'm noticing that DEFF gets used in §4.2 ("Two DEFF estimators bound the non-independence effect") without ever being explicitly defined as "Design Effect."

Looking through the text, DEFF appears repeatedly in the introduction, related work, and limitations sections without expansion, and while it's defined in the appendix, a reader unfamiliar with the term would be confused. This should be flagged as a minor issue — DEFF needs to be expanded to "design effect (DEFF)" on first use in the main text, probably in §4.2 or earlier.
```

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — mcp__agent-ml-research__reviewer_post_to_director · `toolu_01QCj2YhsV4skxucJRGcsM4o`</summary>

```json
{
  "project": "irt_equated_bench_regen",
  "text": "## 模拟 EMNLP 2026 审稿人完整通读评审\n\n---\n\n### 1. 当前估分：3.75（介于 3.5 Borderline Conference 和 4.0 Conference 之间）\n\n**理由**：\n\n论文质量在 borderline 和 accept 的分界线上。最终得分取决于审稿人分配：\n\n- **友好审稿人（关注 benchmark quality / psychometrics）→ 4.0**：问题重要且及时，分析规模空前（5,227 models × 12,508 items × 6 benchmarks），DIF ⊥ web-overlap 的正交性发现真正新颖，控制实验和鲁棒性检验异常充分。\n- **中立审稿人（关注实际影响力）→ 3.5**：\"那又怎样？\" 问题悬而未决——rankings 几乎不变（ρ=0.997），paper 诊断了问题但没给出解决方案。方法本身是教育测量领域 50 年前的 MH 统计量，跨领域移植的新颖性有限。\n- **不利审稿人（偏好算法贡献）→ 3.0**：纯分析论文，无新方法、无新系统、无新数据集。\n\n**维度评分**：\n- Originality: 3.5 — DIF 应用于 LLM benchmarks 新颖；正交性发现重要；但核心方法借用自教育测量，cross-subject matching 的 ΔFPR=0 证明是 trivial by construction\n- Clarity: 3 — 技术精确但密度过高；§4 读起来像技术报告而非叙事；需要 psychometrics 背景才能充分理解\n- Soundness: 3.5 — 极其充分的控制实验（DEFF、random-split、matched-ability、composition control、within-arch）；DEFF 区间诚实；semi-synthetic 框架合理但远离现实\n- Significance: 3 — 问题重要但实际影响不清楚（rankings 不变、因果不明、无 remediation 方案）\n- Presentation: 3 — 排版专业、表格清晰、图表够用；但 §4 过密、Conclusion 过简（3 句）\n\n---\n\n### 2. 从 3.5 到 4.0 的 Gap 分析\n\n#### (a) DDL 前可修复的 writing/framing 问题\n\n1. **Actionability gap（最大弱点）**：§5.2 \"Recommended DIF Audit Protocol\" 停留在抽象层面（\"flagged items warrant mechanism-conditional responses\"）。审稿人最可能的攻击：\"你发现了 31% items 有问题，然后呢？benchmark 维护者具体该做什么？\" 需要一个更具体的 decision flowchart 或至少 3 个可操作步骤。\n\n2. **Conclusion 过于简短**：3 句话、无 future direction、无 practical takeaway。应扩展到 5-6 句，增加 \"what should the community do\" 和 \"what comes next\"。\n\n3. **DEFF 未在主文展开**：主文从未将 \"DEFF\" 展开为 \"design effect\"。仅 Appendix D 标题有 \"Design Effect Estimation\"。不熟悉该术语的审稿人会困惑。应在 §4.2 首次使用时写 \"design effect (DEFF)\"。\n\n4. **Figure 2 无 error bars**：dose-response 图没有展示 5 seeds 的置信区间。这是基本的科学呈现标准。\n\n5. **§4 密度可优化**：§4.2 的 Simpson's paradox 细节（OR reverses from 1.55 to 0.73）、domain invariance 检验（K-W p=0.78）、pseudo R²=0.0007 可以移至 appendix，为 actionability 讨论腾出空间。\n\n#### (b) 需新实验/数据的结构性问题（DDL 前不可行）\n\n1. **因果连接**：将 DIF 连接到具体机制（RLHF、数据规模、memorization）需要已知训练数据的控制实验。\n2. **纵向验证**：证明 DIF-flagged items 预测未来 benchmark 退化需要 temporal holdout validation。\n3. **Remediation 工具**：一个 \"DIF audit dashboard\" 或自动化审计 pipeline 会大幅提升实用价值。\n\n#### (c) 不可控的审稿人主观因素\n\n1. **\"分析论文\"偏好**：部分审稿人倾向接收提出新方法的论文，而非应用现有方法的分析论文。\n2. **Psychometrics 熟悉度**：不熟悉 DIF/IRT/DEFF 术语的审稿人可能低估贡献；反之，教育测量专家可能认为 adaptations 过于浅显。\n3. **Null result 偏见**：DIF ⊥ web-overlap 是 null result。部分审稿人对 null results 持负面态度。\n\n---\n\n### 3. 24h 内可完成的 Top 5 改进（按优先级排序）\n\n**P1. 强化 §5.2 actionability（最高优先级，~1h）**\n- 将 \"mechanism-conditional responses\" 扩展为具体 3-step protocol：\n  (1) C% > 20× random baseline → 触发审计\n  (2) DIF × web-overlap 共现 → retire item；DIF-only → annotate + 监控；direction reversal → 分 cohort 报告\n  (3) 报告 DIF-corrected scores alongside raw scores，引用 equating 文献\n- 这直接回应了 \"so what?\" 这一最可能导致 3.5 而非 4.0 的审稿意见\n\n**P2. 扩展 Conclusion（~30min）**\n- 当前 3 句过于telegraphic。增加 2 句：一句关于 practical recommendation（\"Benchmark maintainers should report DIF-corrected scores alongside raw rankings\"），一句关于 future direction（\"Connecting temporal DIF to specific training factors remains an open causal question\"）\n\n**P3. 主文首次定义 DEFF（~10min）**\n- §4.2 首次出现 \"DEFF\" 时写 \"design effect (DEFF)\"。无此定义，审稿人查 Ctrl+F 找不到展开式。\n\n**P4. Figure 2 添加 error bars（~30min）**\n- 5 seeds 的 std 或 95% CI 应该在 dose-response 图中可见。当前图看起来像单次运行的结果。\n\n**P5. 精简 §4.2 为 actionability 腾空间（~1h）**\n- 将 Simpson's paradox 细节、K-W p=0.78、pseudo R²=0.0007 移至 appendix。这些是 robustness 细节而非核心发现。释放 3-4 行给 §5.2 扩展或 Conclusion 扩展。\n\n---\n\n### 4. R18 预测的新 issue\n\n| 预测 Issue | 概率 | 对评分影响 | 可防御性 |\n|-----------|------|---------|---------|\n| \"Actionability gap — 发现问题没给方案\" | 高 (80%) | -0.5 分 | 可通过 P1 缓解 |\n| \"Single data source (METABENCH/Open LLM Leaderboard)\" | 中 (50%) | -0.25 分 | 部分可防（GSM8K open-ended replication）但根本上无法解决 |\n| \"Binary MCQ 限制 — 不适用于 generation benchmarks\" | 中 (40%) | -0.25 分 | Limitations 已覆盖；GSM8K 提供部分证据 |\n| \"DEFF 区间太宽 (13.5-29.2%，2× factor)\" | 中 (40%) | -0.25 分 | cluster bootstrap 排除下界可部分缓解 |\n| \"Semi-synthetic injection 过于简化\" | 低-中 (30%) | -0.25 分 | difficulty-dependent 变体 + Limitations caveat 已部分回应 |\n| \"为什么用 MH 不用 logistic regression DIF / IRT-based DIF？\" | 低 (20%) | info 级 | §2 已列举替代方法 + nonparametric advantage |\n\n**最危险组合**：如果审稿人同时提出 \"actionability gap\" + \"single data source\"，可能直接给 3.0-3.5。P1 是唯一能在 24h 内显著降低此风险的改进。\n\n---\n\n### 总结\n\n论文在技术严谨性上已经做到了 4.0 级别（控制实验数量和质量远超同类工作），但在 \"impact/actionability\" 维度仍有 gap。P1（§5.2 actionability）和 P2（Conclusion 扩展）是投入产出比最高的改进，估计能将评分期望从 3.75 提升到 ~3.9。"
}
```

</details>

```text
   - Anonymous repo push → in progress
   - Quick-win improvements based on Reviewer feedback → correct priority
   - DDL final submission

4. **GPU budget**: "No GPU needed" — correct, all analyses are CPU-based on existing METABENCH data.

5. **Decision log**: Contains D031-D040, showing the progression from R6 through R17. The ROADMAP is well-structured and reflects the current state accurately.

The priorities are solid — R18 approval, then quick-wins, then final submission. There's a minor lag where the ROADMAP still shows the Reviewer read-through as pending when results are already posted, and the R17 score shows 3.5 instead of my updated 3.75 estimate, but these are just temporal gaps that the Director can address. No directional concerns here.
```

单调递增。具体预测：k≤3 时 ΔFPR<0.02（matching variable 被稀释），k≥10 时 ΔFPR>0.05（matching variable 严重受污）。ΔTPR(k) 在 k=1 与 k=20 之间保持稳定（>0.3 at p=0.5），因为 detection 对 target subject 内的 signal 而非 matching quality 更敏感。\", 288	{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01HettQz7YAmGocaLuQZFJWA","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_0158YeSrcdavq5e6LUf7Uuqb","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/tmp/anon_repo_1779540842/README.md","content":"# IRT-Equated Benchmark Regeneration\n\nCode for the paper: *Psychometric Auditing Reveals Measurement Instability in LLM Benchmarks* (EMNLP 2026 submission).\n\n## Overview\n\nThis repository contains analysis scripts for applying Mantel-Haenszel Differential Item Functioning (MH-DIF) analysis to LLM benchmark evaluation. The methodology treats benchmark items as psychometric test items and detects measurement instability (differential item functioning) across temporal cohorts of language models.\n\n## Repository Structure\n\n### `artifacts/` — Analysis Scripts\n\n| Directory | Description |\n|-----------|-------------|\n| `build_response_matrix.py` | Builds response matrix and metadata from METABENCH MMLU data |\n| `metabench_explore.py` | METABENCH data exploration: cohort sizes, IRT parameters, response matrix statistics |\n| `plan_001/` | Purified MH-DIF detection on MMLU with temporal (2023 vs 2024) cohort comparisons |\n| `plan_001_real_data_dif_mmlu/` | Matched-ability temporal DIF within accuracy quintiles |\n| `plan_001b/` | Matched-ability DIF analysis using temporal comparisons within accuracy quintiles |\n| `plan_004/` | GSM8K replication of MH-DIF methodology with standard and external matching |\n| `plan_010/` | Architecture-stratified DIF analysis on decoder-only models |\n| `plan_011/` | Semi-synthetic DIF validation: injects known contamination and tests detection power |\n| `plan_012/` | IRT theta-based conditioning for MH-DIF stratification |\n| `plan_013/` | Yen's Q3 local independence diagnostic for MMLU items |\n| `plan_013_supplement/` | Cross-analysis of local dependence (Q3) vs DIF concentration |\n| `plan_014/` | Within-subject temporal DIF and cross-subject matching |\n| `plan_015/` | Bifactor-conditioned MH-DIF using per-subject IRT theta |\n| `plan_016/` | Within-subject semi-synthetic injection validation |\n| `plan_016_supplement/` | Bootstrap CIs for ΔFPR in cross-subject matching |\n| `plan_017/` | Downstream impact: model ranking stability from DIF-C item removal |\n| `plan_017_supplement/` | Rank shift distribution and top rank-changing model identification |\n| `plan_018/` | Threshold sensitivity analysis with BH multiple testing correction |\n| `plan_019/` | Instability decomposition into domain-level improvement vs residual instability |\n| `plan_020/` | Circularity ablation comparing DIF-C removal against alternative selection strategies |\n| `plan_021/` | Non-uniform DIF robustness check via logistic regression |\n| `plan_022/` | Model independence sensitivity: three de-duplication levels |\n| `plan_023/` | DIF-C item characterization across difficulty, discrimination, and domain |\n| `plan_024/` | Within-family analysis (Llama-2 vs Llama-3) with paired ability-matched DIF |\n| `plan_026/` | Empirical comparison of DIF-C against Li et al. web-overlap contamination labels |\n| `plan_027_temporal_overlap/` | Temporal DIF signal robustness via cohort ability distribution overlap |\n| `plan_028_bootstrap_ci/` | Bootstrap 95% CI for MH-DIF C% across 6 benchmarks |\n| `plan_028_multi_bench_dif/` | Multi-benchmark temporal MH-DIF (ARC, HellaSwag, WinoGrande, TruthfulQA) |\n| `plan_029_permutation_null/` | Permutation null test for temporal DIF C% (1000 permutations) |\n| `plan_030_odds_ratio/` | Odds ratio and CI analysis for accuracy-DIF regression |\n| `plan_031_temporal_granularity/` | Full-year vs 6-month vs within-year DIF signal comparison |\n| `plan_032_dif_cleaned_mmlu/` | Model ranking changes after removing ETS C-flagged items |\n| `plan_033_subject_dif_concentration/` | Subject-level DIF concentration analysis with domain group summaries |\n| `plan_034_multi_subject_injection/` | Multi-subject simultaneous injection ΔFPR robustness test |\n| `plan_035_q3_inflation_sim/` | Local dependence inflation of MH-DIF Type I error quantification |\n| `deff_parametric_curve/` | Design effect (DEFF) parametric curve fitting vs family size |\n| `difficulty_modeltype_interaction/` | Difficulty × model-type interaction via logistic regression |\n| `k3_power_simulation/` | K=3 vs K=5 strata power simulation for MH-DIF |\n| `lofo_robustness/` | Leave-one-family-out (LOFO) robustness check for temporal DIF C% |\n| `mf1_lofo_power_calibration/` | Power vs composition effect separation in LOFO analysis |\n| `mf2_deff_estimation/` | DEFF estimation with cohort-consistent reference/focal models |\n| `mf2_practical_significance/` | Stable-item Spearman ρ and flip rate across 6 benchmarks |\n| `mf2_rho_degradation/` | Spearman ρ degradation under progressive DIF-C item removal |\n| `mixed_mechanism_semisynthetic/` | Mixed-mechanism semi-synthetic injection (difficulty × model-type) |\n| `predictive_model/` | Item-property predictive model for DIF-C classification |\n| `score_inflation_analysis/` | Score inflation quantification and DIF-corrected scoring |\n| `sf1_cluster_bootstrap/` | Family-level cluster bootstrap CIs for temporal C% |\n| `sf8_power_sensitivity/` | Power sensitivity: TPR as function of item discrimination |\n| `temporal_accumulation/` | C% monotonicity across cumulative temporal distances |\n| `dif_diagnostic.py` | DIF power diagnostic: raw vs BH vs Bonferroni TPR/FPR |\n| `dif_phase2_simulation.py` | Semi-synthetic DIF power simulation using real MMLU IRT parameters |\n| `dif_power_simulation.py` | DIF power Monte Carlo with 2PL IRT and Mantel-Haenszel/LR methods |\n\n#### Rebuttal analyses (`r*` directories)\n\n| Directory | Description |\n|-----------|-------------|\n| `r14_mf1_domain_profile/` | Cross-subject-unique C item concentration with Cohen's kappa |\n| `r15_base_instruct_temporal_dif/` | Within-type temporal DIF (base-only and instruct-only) |\n| `r15_random_split_control/` | Random split control within 2024 cohort as sanity check |\n| `r15_subject_level_rho/` | Subject-level ranking correlation (instability masked by aggregate ρ≈0.997) |\n| `r16_threshold_calibration/` | Random-split null distribution for ETS classification threshold calibration |\n| `r17_evidence/` | Weighted-mean DEFF downstream analysis |\n| `r17_gema_enrichment_ci/` | Bootstrap CI for DIF-C enrichment with MMLU-Redux annotations |\n| `r17_gsm1k_jaccard_permutation/` | GSM1K model-level Jaccard permutation null test |\n| `r17_k_sensitivity/` | K (strata count) sensitivity for MH-DIF (K∈{3,5,7,10,20}) |\n| `r18_base_vs_instruct_dif/` | Base vs instruct temporal MH-DIF with within-type stratification |\n| `r18_dedup_k_curve/` | Family de-duplication C% vs K curve |\n| `r18_icc_by_family_size/` | Per-family homogeneity (mean pairwise Pearson r) vs family size |\n| `r18_rank_bucket_reversal/` | Rank-bucket reversal distribution for closely-ranked models |\n| `r18_restricted_range_rho/` | Restricted-range Spearman ρ for difficulty-DIF direction association |\n| `r19_model_size_dif/` | Model-size DIF decomposition (Small/Medium/Large) |\n| `r19_open_closed_dif/` | Open vs closed-source DIF analysis |\n| `r19_semisynthetic_gradient/` | Difficulty-dependent semi-synthetic injection patterns |\n| `r19_temporal_granularity/` | Half-year cohort pairwise temporal granularity analysis |\n| `r20_size_stratified_deff/` | Size-stratified DEFF with family definition sensitivity |\n| `r22_composition_control/` | Base/instruct composition control via matched subsampling |\n| `r22_deff_simulation/` | DEFF mean vs weighted-mean simulation with Rao-Scott correction |\n| `r22_within_arch_temporal/` | Within-architecture temporal DIF ruling out cross-arch confound |\n\n### `figures/paper/` — Figure Scripts\n\n| Script | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `fig_1_global_vs_differential.py` | Figure 1: Global vs differential measurement comparison |\n| `fig_2_dose_response.py` | Figure 2: Dose-response curve for semi-synthetic injection |\n| `fig_3_within_subject_matching.py` | Figure 3: Within-subject matching results |\n| `fig_4_downstream_impact.py` | Figure 4: Downstream ranking impact |\n| `fig_A1_subject_heatmap.py` | Figure A1: Subject-level DIF heatmap |\n| `fig_A2_q3_distribution.py` | Figure A2: Q3 local dependence distribution |\n| `fig_A3_rho_degradation.py` | Figure A3: Spearman ρ degradation curves |\n\n### `scripts/` — Utility Scripts\n\n| Script | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `r14_cooks_d_analysis.py` | Cook's distance analysis for DIF outlier detection |\n| `r14_domain_profile.py` | Domain-level DIF profiling |\n| `r16_dif_union.py` | Uniform + non-uniform DIF union/intersection sizes |\n\n## Data\n\nRaw data (response matrices, model metadata) is not included due to size.\nTo reproduce:\n1. Download MMLU evaluation results from [MetaBench](https://github.com/ildfrgen/MetaBench) or equivalent source\n2. Run `artifacts/build_response_matrix.py` to construct the response matrix\n3. Run individual analysis scripts in `artifacts/plan_*/`\n\n## Requirements\n\n```\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\nPython 3.9+ recommended.\n\n## License\n\nThis code is released for academic use. See the paper for methodological details.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":662,"cache_read_input_tokens":45887,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":662},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9f6cf3a2-d194-47dc-add5-ca7c2867e53f","uuid":"cfa391fc-9845-4c12-883e-9c881131e22d"}

`r18_base_vs_instruct_dif/` | Base vs instruct temporal MH-DIF with within-type stratification |\n| `r18_dedup_k_curve/` | Family de-duplication C% vs K curve |\n| `r18_icc_by_family_size/` | Per-family homogeneity (mean pairwise Pearson r) vs family size |\n| `r18_rank_bucket_reversal/` | Rank-bucket reversal distribution for closely-ranked models |\n| `r18_restricted_range_rho/` | Restricted-range Spearman ρ for difficulty-DIF direction association |\n| `r19_model_size_dif/` | Model-size DIF decomposition (Small/Medium/Large) |\n| `r19_open_closed_dif/` | Open vs closed-source DIF analysis |\n| `r19_semisynthetic_gradient/` | Difficulty-dependent semi-synthetic injection patterns |\n| `r19_temporal_granularity/` | Half-year cohort pairwise temporal granularity analysis |\n| `r20_size_stratified_deff/` | Size-stratified DEFF with family definition sensitivity |\n| `r22_composition_control/` | Base/instruct composition control via matched subsampling |\n| `r22_deff_simulation/` | DEFF mean vs weighted-mean simulation with Rao-Scott correction |\n| `r22_within_arch_temporal/` | Within-architecture temporal DIF ruling out cross-arch confound |\n\n### `figures/paper/` — Figure Scripts\n\n| Script | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `fig_1_global_vs_differential.py` | Figure 1: Global vs differential measurement comparison |\n| `fig_2_dose_response.py` | Figure 2: Dose-response curve for semi-synthetic injection |\n| `fig_3_within_subject_matching.py` | Figure 3: Within-subject matching results |\n| `fig_4_downstream_impact.py` | Figure 4: Downstream ranking impact |\n| `fig_A1_subject_heatmap.py` | Figure A1: Subject-level DIF heatmap |\n| `fig_A2_q3_distribution.py` | Figure A2: Q3 local dependence distribution |\n| `fig_A3_rho_degradation.py` | Figure A3: Spearman ρ degradation curves |\n\n### `scripts/` — Utility Scripts\n\n| Script | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `r14_cooks_d_analysis.py` | Cook's distance analysis for DIF outlier detection |\n| `r14_domain_profile.py` | Domain-level DIF profiling |\n| `r16_dif_union.py` | Uniform + non-uniform DIF union/intersection sizes |\n\n## Data\n\nRaw data (response matrices, model metadata) is not included due to size.\nTo reproduce:\n1. Download MMLU evaluation results from [MetaBench](https://github.com/ildfrgen/MetaBench) or equivalent source\n2. Run `artifacts/build_response_matrix.py` to construct the response matrix\n3. Run individual analysis scripts in `artifacts/plan_*/`\n\n## Requirements\n\n```\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\nPython 3.9+ recommended.\n\n## License\n\nThis code is released for academic use. See the paper for methodological details.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

IRT-Equated Benchmark Regeneration\\n\\nCode for the paper: *Psychometric Auditing Reveals Measurement Instability in LLM Benchmarks* (EMNLP 2026 submission).\\n\\n## Overview\\n\\nThis repository contains analysis scripts for applying Mantel-Haenszel Differential Item Functioning (MH-DIF) analysis to LLM benchmark evaluation. The methodology treats benchmark items as psychometric test items and detects measurement instability (differential item functioning) across temporal cohorts of language models.\\n\\n## Repository Structure\\n\\n### `artifacts/` — Analysis Scripts\\n\\n| Directory | Description |\\n|-----------|-------------|\\n| `build_response_matrix.py` | Builds response matrix and metadata from METABENCH MMLU data |\\n| `metabench_explore.py` | METABENCH data exploration: cohort sizes, IRT parameters, response matrix statistics |\\n| `plan_001/` | Purified MH-DIF detection on MMLU with temporal (2023 vs 2024) cohort comparisons |\\n| `plan_001_real_data_dif_mmlu/` | Matched-ability temporal DIF within accuracy quintiles |\\n| `plan_001b/` | Matched-ability DIF analysis using temporal comparisons within accuracy quintiles |\\n| `plan_004/` | GSM8K replication of MH-DIF methodology with standard and external matching |\\n| `plan_010/` | Architecture-stratified DIF analysis on decoder-only models |\\n| `plan_011/` | Semi-synthetic DIF validation: injects known contamination and tests detection power |\\n| `plan_012/` | IRT theta-based conditioning for MH-DIF stratification |\\n| `plan_013/` | Yen's Q3 local independence diagnostic for MMLU items |\\n| `plan_013_supplement/` | Cross-analysis of local dependence (Q3) vs DIF concentration |\\n| `plan_014/` | Within-subject temporal DIF and cross-subject matching |\\n| `plan_015/` | Bifactor-conditioned MH-DIF using per-subject IRT theta |\\n| `plan_016/` | Within-subject semi-synthetic injection validation |\\n| `plan_016_supplement/` | Bootstrap CIs for ΔFPR in cross-subject matching |\\n| `plan_017/` | Downstream impact: model ranking stability from DIF-C item removal |\\n| `plan_017_supplement/` | Rank shift distribution and top rank-changing model identification |\\n| `plan_018/` | Threshold sensitivity analysis with BH multiple testing correction |\\n| `plan_019/` | Instability decomposition into domain-level improvement vs residual instability |\\n| `plan_020/` | Circularity ablation comparing DIF-C removal against alternative selection strategies |\\n| `plan_021/` | Non-uniform DIF robustness check via logistic regression |\\n| `plan_022/` | Model independence sensitivity: three de-duplication levels |\\n| `plan_023/` | DIF-C item characterization across difficulty, discrimination, and domain |\\n| `plan_024/` | Within-family analysis (Llama-2 vs Llama-3) with paired ability-matched DIF |\\n| `plan_026/` | Empirical comparison of DIF-C against Li et al. web-overlap contamination labels |\\n| `plan_027_temporal_overlap/` | Temporal DIF signal robustness via cohort ability distribution overlap |\\n| `plan_028_bootstrap_ci/` | Bootstrap 95% CI for MH-DIF C% across 6 benchmarks |\\n| `plan_028_multi_bench_dif/` | Multi-benchmark temporal MH-DIF (ARC, HellaSwag, WinoGrande, TruthfulQA) |\\n| `plan_029_permutation_null/` | Permutation null test for temporal DIF C% (1000 permutations) |\\n| `plan_030_odds_ratio/` | Odds ratio and CI analysis for accuracy-DIF regression |\\n| `plan_031_temporal_granularity/` | Full-year vs 6-month vs within-year DIF signal comparison |\\n| `plan_032_dif_cleaned_mmlu/` | Model ranking changes after removing ETS C-flagged items |\\n| `plan_033_subject_dif_concentration/` | Subject-level DIF concentration analysis with domain group summaries |\\n| `plan_034_multi_subject_injection/` | Multi-subject simultaneous injection ΔFPR robustness test |\\n| `plan_035_q3_inflation_sim/` | Local dependence inflation of MH-DIF Type I error quantification |\\n| `deff_parametric_curve/` | Design effect (DEFF) parametric curve fitting vs family size |\\n| `difficulty_modeltype_interaction/` | Difficulty × model-type interaction via logistic regression |\\n| `k3_power_simulation/` | K=3 vs K=5 strata power simulation for MH-DIF |\\n| `lofo_robustness/` | Leave-one-family-out (LOFO) robustness check for temporal DIF C% |\\n| `mf1_lofo_power_calibration/` | Power vs composition effect separation in LOFO analysis |\\n| `mf2_deff_estimation/` | DEFF estimation with cohort-consistent reference/focal models |\\n| `mf2_practical_significance/` | Stable-item Spearman ρ and flip rate across 6 benchmarks |\\n| `mf2_rho_degradation/` | Spearman ρ degradation under progressive DIF-C item removal |\\n| `mixed_mechanism_semisynthetic/` | Mixed-mechanism semi-synthetic injection (difficulty × model-type) |\\n| `predictive_model/` | Item-property predictive model for DIF-C classification |\\n| `score_inflation_analysis/` | Score inflation quantification and DIF-corrected scoring |\\n| `sf1_cluster_bootstrap/` | Family-level cluster bootstrap CIs for temporal C% |\\n| `sf8_power_sensitivity/` | Power sensitivity: TPR as function of item discrimination |\\n| `temporal_accumulation/` | C% monotonicity across cumulative temporal distances |\\n| `dif_diagnostic.py` | DIF power diagnostic: raw vs BH vs Bonferroni TPR/FPR |\\n| `dif_phase2_simulation.py` | Semi-synthetic DIF power simulation using real MMLU IRT parameters |\\n| `dif_power_simulation.py` | DIF power Monte Carlo with 2PL IRT and Mantel-Haenszel/LR methods |\\n\\n#### Rebuttal analyses (`r*` directories)\\n\\n| Directory | Description |\\n|-----------|-------------|\\n| `r14_mf1_domain_profile/` | Cross-subject-unique C item concentration with Cohen's kappa |\\n| `r15_base_instruct_temporal_dif/` | Within-type temporal DIF (base-only and instruct-only) |\\n| `r15_random_split_control/` | Random split control within 2024 cohort as sanity check |\\n| `r15_subject_level_rho/` | Subject-level ranking correlation (instability masked by aggregate ρ≈0.997) |\\n| `r16_threshold_calibration/` | Random-split null distribution for ETS classification threshold calibration |\\n| `r17_evidence/` | Weighted-mean DEFF downstream analysis |\\n| `r17_gema_enrichment_ci/` | Bootstrap CI for DIF-C enrichment with MMLU-Redux annotations |\\n| `r17_gsm1k_jaccard_permutation/` | GSM1K model-level Jaccard permutation null test |\\n| `r17_k_sensitivity/` | K (strata count) sensitivity for MH-DIF (K∈{3,5,7,10,20}) |\\n| `r18_base_vs_instruct_dif/` | Base vs instruct temporal MH-DIF with within-type stratification |\\n| `r18_dedup_k_curve/` | Family de-duplication C% vs K curve |\\n| `r18_icc_by_family_size/` | Per-family homogeneity (mean pairwise Pearson r) vs family size |\\n| `r18_rank_bucket_reversal/` | Rank-bucket reversal distribution for closely-ranked models |\\n| `r18_restricted_range_rho/` | Restricted-range Spearman ρ for difficulty-DIF direction association |\\n| `r19_model_size_dif/` | Model-size DIF decomposition (Small/Medium/Large) |\\n| `r19_open_closed_dif/` | Open vs closed-source DIF analysis |\\n| `r19_semisynthetic_gradient/` | Difficulty-dependent semi-synthetic injection patterns |\\n| `r19_temporal_granularity/` | Half-year cohort pairwise temporal granularity analysis |\\n| `r20_size_stratified_deff/` | Size-stratified DEFF with family definition sensitivity |\\n| `r22_composition_control/` | Base/instruct composition control via matched subsampling |\\n| `r22_deff_simulation/` | DEFF mean vs weighted-mean simulation with Rao-Scott correction |\\n| `r22_within_arch_temporal/` | Within-architecture temporal DIF ruling out cross-arch confound |\\n\\n### `figures/paper/` — Figure Scripts\\n\\n| Script | Description |\\n|--------|-------------|\\n| `fig_1_global_vs_differential.py` | Figure 1: Global vs differential measurement comparison |\\n| `fig_2_dose_response.py` | Figure 2: Dose-response curve for semi-synthetic injection |\\n| `fig_3_within_subject_matching.py` | Figure 3: Within-subject matching results |\\n| `fig_4_downstream_impact.py` | Figure 4: Downstream ranking impact |\\n| `fig_A1_subject_heatmap.py` | Figure A1: Subject-level DIF heatmap |\\n| `fig_A2_q3_distribution.py` | Figure A2: Q3 local dependence distribution |\\n| `fig_A3_rho_degradation.py` | Figure A3: Spearman ρ degradation curves |\\n\\n### `scripts/` — Utility Scripts\\n\\n| Script | Description |\\n|--------|-------------|\\n| `r14_cooks_d_analysis.py` | Cook's distance analysis for DIF outlier detection |\\n| `r14_domain_profile.py` | Domain-level DIF profiling |\\n| `r16_dif_union.py` | Uniform + non-uniform DIF union/intersection sizes |\\n\\n## Data\\n\\nRaw data (response matrices, model metadata) is not included due to size.\\nTo reproduce:\\n1. Download MMLU evaluation results from [MetaBench](https://github.com/ildfrgen/MetaBench) or equivalent source\\n2. Run `artifacts/build_response_matrix.py` to construct the response matrix\\n3. Run individual analysis scripts in `artifacts/plan_*/`\\n\\n## Requirements\\n\\n```\\npip install -r requirements.txt\\n```\\n\\nPython 3.9+ recommended.\\n\\n## License\\n\\nThis code is released for academic use. web-overlap contamination labels |\\n| `plan_027_temporal_overlap/` | Temporal DIF signal robustness via cohort ability distribution overlap |\\n| `plan_028_bootstrap_ci/` | Bootstrap 95% CI for MH-DIF C% across 6 benchmarks |\\n| `plan_028_multi_bench_dif/` | Multi-benchmark temporal MH-DIF (ARC, HellaSwag, WinoGrande, TruthfulQA) |\\n| `plan_029_permutation_null/` | Permutation null test for temporal DIF C% (1000 permutations) |\\n| `plan_030_odds_ratio/` | Odds ratio and CI analysis for accuracy-DIF regression |\\n| `plan_031_temporal_granularity/` | Full-year vs 6-month vs within-year DIF signal comparison |\\n| `plan_032_dif_cleaned_mmlu/` | Model ranking changes after removing ETS C-flagged items |\\n| `plan_033_subject_dif_concentration/` | Subject-level DIF concentration analysis with domain group summaries |\\n| `plan_034_multi_subject_injection/` | Multi-subject simultaneous injection ΔFPR robustness test |\\n| `plan_035_q3_inflation_sim/` | Local dependence inflation of MH-DIF Type I error quantification |\\n| `deff_parametric_curve/` | Design effect (DEFF) parametric curve fitting vs family size |\\n| `difficulty_modeltype_interaction/` | Difficulty × model-type interaction via logistic regression |\\n| `k3_power_simulation/` | K=3 vs K=5 strata power simulation for MH-DIF |\\n| `lofo_robustness/` | Leave-one-family-out (LOFO) robustness check for temporal DIF C% |\\n| `mf1_lofo_power_calibration/` | Power vs composition effect separation in LOFO analysis |\\n| `mf2_deff_estimation/` | DEFF estimation with cohort-consistent reference/focal models |\\n| `mf2_practical_significance/` | Stable-item Spearman ρ and flip rate across 6 benchmarks |\\n| `mf2_rho_degradation/` | Spearman ρ degradation under progressive DIF-C item removal |\\n| `mixed_mechanism_semisynthetic/` | Mixed-mechanism semi-synthetic injection (difficulty × model-type) |\\n| `predictive_model/` | Item-property predictive model for DIF-C classification |\\n| `score_inflation_analysis/` | Score inflation quantification and DIF-corrected scoring |\\n| `sf1_cluster_bootstrap/` | Family-level cluster bootstrap CIs for temporal C% |\\n| `sf8_power_sensitivity/` | Power sensitivity: TPR as function of item discrimination |\\n| `temporal_accumulation/` | C% monotonicity across cumulative temporal distances |\\n| `dif_diagnostic.py` | DIF power diagnostic: raw vs BH vs Bonferroni TPR/FPR |\\n| `dif_phase2_simulation.py` | Semi-synthetic DIF power simulation using real MMLU IRT parameters |\\n| `dif_power_simulation.py` | DIF power Monte Carlo with 2PL IRT and Mantel-Haenszel/LR methods |\\n\\n#### Rebuttal analyses (`r*` directories)\\n\\n| Directory | Description |\\n|-----------|-------------|\\n| `r14_mf1_domain_profile/` | Cross-subject-unique C item concentration with Cohen's kappa |\\n| `r15_base_instruct_temporal_dif/` | Within-type temporal DIF (base-only and instruct-only) |\\n| `r15_random_split_control/` | Random split control within 2024 cohort as sanity check |\\n| `r15_subject_level_rho/` | Subject-level ranking correlation (instability masked by aggregate ρ≈0.997) |\\n| `r16_threshold_calibration/` | Random-split null distribution for ETS classification threshold calibration |\\n| `r17_evidence/` | Weighted-mean DEFF downstream analysis |\\n| `r17_gema_enrichment_ci/` | Bootstrap CI for DIF-C enrichment with MMLU-Redux annotations |\\n| `r17_gsm1k_jaccard_permutation/` | GSM1K model-level Jaccard permutation null test |\\n| `r17_k_sensitivity/` | K (strata count) sensitivity for MH-DIF (K∈{3,5,7,10,20}) |\\n| `r18_base_vs_instruct_dif/` | Base vs instruct temporal MH-DIF with within-type stratification |\\n| `r18_dedup_k_curve/` | Family de-duplication C% vs K curve |\\n| `r18_icc_by_family_size/` | Per-family homogeneity (mean pairwise Pearson r) vs family size |\\n| `r18_rank_bucket_reversal/` | Rank-bucket reversal distribution for closely-ranked models |\\n| `r18_restricted_range_rho/` | Restricted-range Spearman ρ for difficulty-DIF direction association |\\n| `r19_model_size_dif/` | Model-size DIF decomposition (Small/Medium/Large) |\\n| `r19_open_closed_dif/` | Open vs closed-source DIF analysis |\\n| `r19_semisynthetic_gradient/` | Difficulty-dependent semi-synthetic injection patterns |\\n| `r19_temporal_granularity/` | Half-year cohort pairwise temporal granularity analysis |\\n| `r20_size_stratified_deff/` | Size-stratified DEFF with family definition sensitivity |\\n| `r22_composition_control/` | Base/instruct composition control via matched subsampling |\\n| `r22_deff_simulation/` | DEFF mean vs weighted-mean simulation with Rao-Scott correction |\\n| `r22_within_arch_temporal/` | Within-architecture temporal DIF ruling out cross-arch confound |\\n\\n### `figures/paper/` — Figure Scripts\\n\\n| Script | Description |\\n|--------|-------------|\\n| `fig_1_global_vs_differential.py` | Figure 1: Global vs
